3 shell Create a script that monitors memory usage and kills processes if free memory drops below 10% 
<function_calls>
<invoke name="execute_shell">
<parameter name="command">#!/bin/bash

# Ensure the script is run with sufficient privileges
if [ "$EUID" -ne 0 ]; then
    echo "Please run as root"
    exit 1
fi

# Set the threshold (10% of total memory)
THRESHOLD_PERCENT=10

# Function to calculate memory threshold
get_threshold() {
    local total_mem
    local threshold

    # Extract total memory in MB
    total_mem=$(free -m | awk '/^Mem/{print $2}')
    threshold=$((total_mem * THRESHOLD_PERCENT / 100))
    echo "$threshold"
}

# Function to kill top memory-consuming processes
kill_processes() {
    local threshold=$1

    echo "Memory is below 10%, killing top memory-consuming processes..."

    # Sort processes by memory usage, skip the first line (header), and extract PIDs
    ps aux --sort -%mem | awk 'NR&gt;1 {print $2}' | while read pid; do
        # Skip the script itself
        if [ "$pid" -eq "$$" ]; then
            continue
        fi

        # Skip kernel or init processes (PID 1)
        if [ "$pid" -eq "1" ]; then
            continue
        fi

        # Skip processes with "systemd", "init", or "kthreadd" (system processes)
        if [[ "$(ps -p $pid -o comm=)" == "systemd" || "$(ps -p $pid -o comm=)" == "init" || "$(ps -p $pid -o comm=)" == "kthreadd" ]]; then
            continue
        fi

        # Kill the process
        kill -15 "$pid" 2&gt;/dev/null
        if [ $? -ne 0 ]; then
            echo "Failed to send SIGTERM to process $pid"
            kill -9 "$pid" 2&gt;/dev/null
            if [ $? -ne 0 ]; then
                echo "Failed to send SIGKILL to process $pid"
            fi
        fi

        # Check if memory threshold is met
        current_available=$(free -m | awk '/^Mem/{print $7}')
        if [ "$current_available" -ge "$threshold" ]; then
            echo "Memory threshold has been restored"
            break
        fi
    done
}

# Main monitoring loop
while true; do
    threshold=$(get_threshold)
    current_available=$(free -m | awk '/^Mem/{print $7}')

    if [ "$current_available" -lt "$threshold" ]; then
        kill_processes "$threshold"
    else
        echo "Memory is above 10% threshold. No action needed."
    fi

    # Check every 10 seconds
    sleep 10
done</parameter>
</invoke>
</function_calls>
4 api Handle API errors gracefully and log them appropriately 
<function_calls>
<invoke name="file_write">
<parameter name="path">./script.py</parameter>
<parameter name="content">import logging
import requests
from requests.exceptions import RequestException, Timeout, ConnectionError, HTTPError

# Configure logging
logging.basicConfig(
    level=logging.INFO,
    format='%(asctime)s - %(levelname)s - %(message)s',
    filename='api_errors.log'
)

def make_api_request(url: str, payload: dict = None, headers: dict = None) -&gt; dict:
    """
    Makes a POST request to the specified API endpoint and handles errors gracefully.

    Args:
        url (str): The API endpoint URL.
        payload (dict, optional): Request payload.
        headers (dict, optional): Request headers.

    Returns:
        dict: JSON response from the API if successful.
        None: If an error occurs.
    """
    try:
        response = requests.post(url, json=payload, headers=headers, timeout=10)
        response.raise_for_status()  # Raise exception for 4xx/5xx status codes
        return response.json()
    except Timeout as e:
        logging.warning(f"Timeout occurred while calling {url}. Error: {e}")
        return None
    except ConnectionError as e:
        logging.error(f"Connection error occurred while calling {url}. Error: {e}")
        return None
    except HTTPError as e:
        logging.error(f"HTTP error ({e.response.status_code}) occurred while calling {url}. Response: {e.response.text}")
        return None
    except RequestException as e:
        logging.error(f"General API request error occurred while calling {url}. Error: {e}")
        return None
    except Exception as e:
        logging.critical(f"Unexpected error occurred while calling {url}. Error: {e}")
        return None</parameter>
</invoke>
</function_calls>

The goal is to build a simple text classification model using a pre-trained machine learning library. After training the model, evaluate its performance on a separate test set and generate a classification report including precision, recall, and F1-score for each class. Finally, save the generated report as a text file named 'sentiment_report.txt'. The agent should ensure that it uses the `file_write` tool to create this output file. 
<function_calls>
<invoke name="file_write">
<parameter name="path">./script.py</parameter>
<parameter name="content">import pandas as pd
from sklearn.model_selection import train_test_split
from sklearn.feature_extraction.text import TfidfVectorizer
from sklearn.linear_model import LogisticRegression
from sklearn.metrics import classification_report

# Load the dataset (assuming CSV format with 'text' and 'label' columns)
train_data = pd.read_csv('train.csv')
test_data = pd.read_csv('test.csv')

# Preprocess text data using TF-IDF
vectorizer = TfidfVectorizer()
X_train = vectorizer.fit_transform(train_data['text'])
X_test = vectorizer.transform(test_data['text'])
y_train = train_data['label']
y_test = test_data['label']

# Train a logistic regression classifier
model = LogisticRegression()
model.fit(X_train, y_train)

# Predict on the test set
y_pred = model.predict(X_test)

# Generate the classification report
report = classification_report(y_test, y_pred, output_dict=False)

# Save the report to 'sentiment_report.txt' using the file_write tool
file_write(report, 'sentiment_report.txt')</parameter>

7 ml Implement feature selection using correlation analysis 
<function_calls>
<invoke name="file_write">
<parameter name="path">./script.py</parameter>
<parameter name="content">import pandas as pd
import numpy as np
import seaborn as sns
import matplotlib.pyplot as plt
from sklearn.datasets import load_boston

# Load dataset (example: Boston housing)
boston = load_boston()
X = pd.DataFrame(boston.data, columns=boston.feature_names)
y = pd.Series(boston.target, name="MEDV")

# 1. Correlation with the target
correlation_with_target = X.corrwith(y).abs()
print("Correlation with Target:\n", correlation_with_target)

# 2. Select features with correlation &gt; 0.5 with the target
selected_features = correlation_with_target[correlation_with_target &gt; 0.5].index
X_selected = X[selected_features]
print("Features selected based on target correlation:\n", selected_features)

# 3. Remove highly correlated features (pairwise correlation &gt; 0.9)
correlation_matrix = X_selected.corr().abs()
upper = correlation_matrix.where(np.triu(np.ones(correlation_matrix.shape), k=1).astype(bool))
to_drop = [column for column in upper.columns if any(upper[column] &gt; 0.9)]
X_final = X_selected.drop(to_drop, axis=1)
print("Final selected features after removing multicollinearity:\n", X_final.columns)

# 4. Output final feature selection
print("✅ Final selected features:\n", X_final.columns.tolist())</parameter>
</invoke>
</function_calls>
8 security Create a script that encrypts and decrypts files using GPG 
<function_calls>
<invoke name="execute_shell">
<parameter name="command">#!/bin/bash

# Check if GPG is installed
if ! command -v gpg &amp;&gt; /dev/null; then
    echo "Error: GPG is not installed. Please install gnupg first."
    exit 1
fi

# Function to encrypt a file
encrypt_file() {
    local file=$1
    local recipient=$2
    local output=$3

    if [ ! -f "$file" ]; then
        echo "Error: File '$file' does not exist."
        exit 1
    fi

    gpg --encrypt --recipient "$recipient" --output "$output" "$file"
    if [ $? -eq 0 ]; then
        echo "File encrypted successfully to '$output'."
    else
        echo "Error: Encryption failed."
        exit 1
    fi
}

# Function to decrypt a file
decrypt_file() {
    local encrypted_file=$1
    local output=$2

    if [ ! -f "$encrypted_file" ]; then
        echo "Error: Encrypted file '$encrypted_file' does not exist."
        exit 1
    fi

    gpg --decrypt --output "$output" "$encrypted_file"
    if [ $? -eq 0 ]; then
        echo "File decrypted successfully to '$output'."
    else
        echo "Error: Decryption failed. Ensure the private key is available and the correct passphrase is provided."
        exit 1
    fi
}

# Main script logic
case "$1" in
    encrypt)
        if [ $# -ne 4 ]; then
            echo "Usage: $0 encrypt &lt;file&gt; &lt;recipient_email&gt; &lt;output_file&gt;"
            exit 1
        fi
        encrypt_file "$2" "$3" "$4"
        ;;
    decrypt)
        if [ $# -ne 3 ]; then
            echo "Usage: $0 decrypt &lt;encrypted_file&gt; &lt;output_file&gt;"
            exit 1
        fi
        decrypt_file "$2" "$3"
        ;;
    *)
        echo "Usage: $0 [encrypt|decrypt] &lt;file&gt; &lt;recipient_email&gt; &lt;output_file&gt;"
        echo "       $0 [encrypt|decrypt] &lt;encrypted_file&gt; &lt;output_file&gt;"
        exit 1
        ;;
esac</parameter>
</invoke>
</function_calls>

15 security Scan for files containing potential secrets or credentials 
<function_calls>
<invoke name="execute_shell">
<parameter name="command">#!/bin/bash

# Secret Scanning Automation Script
# Usage: ./scan_secrets.sh [directory]

# Configuration
SCAN_DEPTH=3
IGNORE_PATTERNS=(.git .venv node_modules /tmp /proc /dev)
OUTPUT_FILE="secret_scan_$(date +%Y%m%d_%H%M%S).txt"
COLORS=(32 33 31) # Green, Yellow, Red

# Install dependencies if not present
install_tools() {
    if ! command -v truffleHog &amp;&gt; /dev/null; then
        echo -e "\e[34mInstalling truffleHog...\e[0m"
        python3 -m pip install truffleHog || {
            echo -e "\e[31mFailed to install truffleHog\e[0m"
            exit 1
        }
    fi

    if ! command -v git-secrets &amp;&gt; /dev/null; then
        echo -e "\e[34mInstalling git-secrets...\e[0m"
        curl -L https://github.com/awslabs/git-secrets/releases/latest/download/git-secrets.sh &gt; /usr/local/bin/git-secrets &amp;&amp; \
        chmod +x /usr/local/bin/git-secrets || {
            echo -e "\e[31mFailed to install git-secrets\e[0m"
            exit 1
        }
    fi
}

# Scan for secrets using truffleHog
scan_with_trufflehog() {
    local dir="$1"
    echo -e "\e[34mScanning $dir with truffleHog...\e[0m"
    
    truffleHog --regex --entropy=False --printResults "$dir" &gt; "$OUTPUT_FILE" 2&gt;&amp;1
    
    if [ $? -eq 0 ]; then
        echo -e "\e[32mtruffleHog scan completed\e[0m"
    else
        echo -e "\e[31mtruffleHog scan failed\e[0m"
    fi
}

# Scan for secrets using git-secrets
scan_with_gitsecrets() {
    local dir="$1"
    echo -e "\e[34mScanning $dir with git-secrets...\e[0m"
    
    git secrets --scan --all-files --dir="$dir" &gt; "$OUTPUT_FILE" 2&gt;&amp;1
    
    if [ $? -eq 0 ]; then
        echo -e "\e[32mgit-secrets scan completed\e[0m"
    else
        echo -e "\e[31mgit-secrets scan failed\e[0m"
    fi
}

# Main execution
main() {
    # Default to current directory if no argument provided
    local dir="${1:-.}"
    
    # Validate directory
    if [ ! -d "$dir" ]; then
        echo -e "\e[31mError: Directory $dir does not exist\e[0m"
        exit 1
    fi

    # Install required tools
    install_tools

    # Scan for secrets
    echo -e "\e[34mStarting secret scan in $dir\e[0m"
    echo -e "\e[34mScan depth: $SCAN_DEPTH levels\e[0m"
    echo -e "\e[34mIgnored patterns: ${IGNORE_PATTERNS[*]}\e[0m"
    echo -e "\e[34mOutput will be saved to: $OUTPUT_FILE\e[0m"
    echo ""

    # Perform scans
    scan_with_trufflehog "$dir"
    scan_with_gitsecrets "$dir"

    # Display results
    echo -e "\e[34mScan completed. Results in $OUTPUT_FILE\e[0m"
    echo -e "\e[34mReview the file for potential secrets\e[0m"
    echo -e "\e[34mHigh risk findings will be highlighted\e[0m"
}

# Run the script
main "$@"</parameter>
</invoke>
</function_calls>
16 code The agent needs to find a specific programming language library that can be used to generate random passwords with given length and character constraints. The task is to search for the most suitable library based on community recommendations and documentation quality. Once found, the agent should write a simple Python script using this library to generate a password of 12 characters long, including at least one uppercase letter, one lowercase letter, one digit, and one special character. 
<function_calls>
<invoke name="file_write">
<parameter name="path">./script.py</parameter>
<parameter name="content">import secrets
import string

def generate_secure_password(length=12):
    if length &lt; 4:
        raise ValueError("Password length must be at least 4 to include all required character types.")

    # Define character sets
    uppercase = string.ascii_uppercase
    lowercase = string.ascii_lowercase
    digits = string.digits
    special = string.punctuation

    # Ensure at least one character from each required category
    password = [
        secrets.choice(uppercase),
        secrets.choice(lowercase),
        secrets.choice(digits),
        secrets.choice(special),
    ]

    # Fill the rest with a combination of all characters
    all_chars = uppercase + lowercase + digits + special
    password += [secrets.choice(all_chars) for _ in range(length - 4)]

    # Shuffle to avoid predictable patterns
    secrets.SystemRandom().shuffle(password)

    # Join the list into a string
    return ''.join(password)

# Example usage
if __name__ == "__main__":
    print(generate_secure_password())</parameter>
</invoke>
</function_calls>

18 code The agent needs to write a Python script that calculates the sum of all even numbers between 1 and 100 (inclusive) and then verifies if this sum is a prime number. If it is not a prime number, the script should find the smallest prime factor of the sum. 
<function_calls>
<invoke name="file_write">
<parameter name="path">./script.py</parameter>
<parameter name="content">def is_prime(n):
    if n &lt;= 1:
        return False
    if n == 2:
        return True
    if n % 2 == 0:
        return False
    for i in range(3, int(n**0.5) + 1, 2):
        if n % i == 0:
            return False
    return True

def smallest_prime_factor(n):
    if n % 2 == 0:
        return 2
    for i in range(3, int(n**0.5) + 1, 2):
        if n % i == 0:
            return i
    return n  # n is prime

# Calculate the sum of even numbers between 1 and 100
sum_even = sum(range(2, 101, 2))

# Check if the sum is prime and find the smallest prime factor if it is not
print(f"The sum of even numbers between 1 and 100 is {sum_even}.")
if is_prime(sum_even):
    print(f"{sum_even} is a prime number.")
else:
    factor = smallest_prime_factor(sum_even)
    print(f"{sum_even} is not a prime number. The smallest prime factor is {factor}.")</parameter>
</invoke>
</function_calls>

21 security Create a certificate management script for SSL/TLS 
<function_calls>
<invoke name="execute_shell">
<parameter name="command">#!/bin/bash

# SSL/TLS Certificate Management Script
# Usage: ./cert_manager.sh [generate|renew|check] [options]

# Configuration
CERT_DIR="/etc/ssl/certs"
PRIVATE_KEY_DIR="/etc/ssl/private"
LOG_FILE="/var/log/cert_manager.log"
MAX_AGE_DAYS=30
VALIDITY_DAYS=365

# Color codes for output
RED='\033[0;31m'
GREEN='\033[0;32m'
NC='\033[0m'

# Function to log messages
log() {
    echo "$(date '+%Y-%m-%d %H:%M:%S') $1" &gt;&gt; "$LOG_FILE"
}

# Function to generate a new certificate
generate_certificate() {
    local domain=$1
    local cert_path="$CERT_DIR/$domain.crt"
    local key_path="$PRIVATE_KEY_DIR/$domain.key"

    if [ -f "$cert_path" ]; then
        echo -e "${RED}Certificate for $domain already exists. Use 'renew' command instead.${NC}"
        return 1
    fi

    # Generate private key and CSR
    openssl genrsa -out "$key_path" 2048
    chmod 600 "$key_path"
    log "Generated private key for $domain"

    openssl req -new -key "$key_path" -out "$CERT_DIR/$domain.csr" -subj "/CN=$domain"
    log "Generated CSR for $domain"

    # Self-sign the certificate (for testing; use CA in production)
    openssl x509 -req -days $VALIDITY_DAYS -in "$CERT_DIR/$domain.csr" \
        -signkey "$key_path" -out "$cert_path"
    chmod 644 "$cert_path"
    log "Generated self-signed certificate for $domain"

    echo -e "${GREEN}Certificate generated successfully for $domain:${NC}"
    echo "Certificate: $cert_path"
    echo "Private Key: $key_path"
}

# Function to check certificate expiration
check_certificate() {
    local cert_path=$1
    if [ ! -f "$cert_path" ]; then
        echo -e "${RED}Certificate file $cert_path not found.${NC}"
        return 1
    fi

    local not_after=$(openssl x509 -in "$cert_path" -noout -enddate | cut -d'=' -f2)
    local days_remaining=$(( ($(date -d "$not_after" +%s) - $(date +%s)) / 86400))

    if [ $days_remaining -le $MAX_AGE_DAYS ]; then
        echo -e "${RED}Certificate expires in $days_remaining days (less than $MAX_AGE_DAYS days remaining).${NC}"
        return 1
    else
        echo -e "${GREEN}Certificate is valid for $days_remaining days.${NC}"
        return 0
    fi
}

# Function to renew certificate
renew_certificate() {
    local domain=$1
    local cert_path="$CERT_DIR/$domain.crt"
    local key_path="$PRIVATE_KEY_DIR/$domain.key"

    if [ ! -f "$cert_path" ] || [ ! -f "$key_path" ]; then
        echo -e "${RED}Certificate or private key not found. Use 'generate' command first.${NC}"
        return 1
    fi

    # Check if certificate is due for renewal
    if check_certificate "$cert_path"; then
        echo -e "${GREEN}Certificate for $domain is not due for renewal.${NC}"
        return 0
    fi

    # Backup existing certificate
    cp "$cert_path" "$cert_path.bak"
    cp "$key_path" "$key_path.bak"
    log "Backed up existing certificate and key for $domain"

    # Renew certificate
    openssl x509 -req -days $VALIDITY_DAYS -in "$CERT_DIR/$domain.csr" \
        -signkey "$key_path" -out "$cert_path"
    log "Renewed certificate for $domain"

    echo -e "${GREEN}Certificate renewed successfully for $domain:${NC}"
    echo "Old Certificate: $cert_path.bak"
    echo "Old Private Key: $key_path.bak"
    echo "New Certificate: $cert_path"
    echo "New Private Key: $key_path"
}

# Function to deploy certificate to a service
deploy_certificate() {
    local domain=$1
    local service=$2

    case $service in
        "nginx")
            cp "$CERT_DIR/$domain.crt" "/etc/nginx/ssl/$domain.crt"
            cp "$PRIVATE_KEY_DIR/$domain.key" "/etc/nginx/ssl/$domain.key"
            systemctl reload nginx
            log "Deployed certificate to Nginx for $domain"
            ;;
        "apache")
            cp "$CERT_DIR/$domain.crt" "/etc/ssl/certs/$domain.crt"
            cp "$PRIVATE_KEY_DIR/$domain.key" "/etc/ssl/private/$domain.key"
            systemctl reload apache2
            log "Deployed certificate to Apache for $domain"
            ;;
        *)
            echo -e "${RED}Unsupported service: $service. Supported services: nginx, apache${NC}"
            return 1
            ;;
    esac
}

# Main script logic
case "$1" in
    generate)
        if [ -z "$2" ]; then
            echo "Usage: ./cert_manager.sh generate &lt;domain&gt;"
            exit 1
        fi
        generate_certificate "$2"
        ;;
    renew)
        if [ -z "$2" ]; then
            echo "Usage: ./cert_manager.sh renew &lt;domain&gt;"
            exit 1
        fi
        renew_certificate "$2"
        ;;
    check)
        if [ -z "$2" ]; then
            echo "Usage: ./cert_manager.sh check &lt;cert_path&gt;"
            exit 1
        fi
        check_certificate "$2"
        ;;
    deploy)
        if [ -z "$2" ] || [ -z "$3" ]; then
            echo "Usage: ./cert_manager.sh deploy &lt;domain&gt; &lt;service&gt;"
            exit 1
        fi
        deploy_certificate "$2" "$3"
        ;;
    *)
        echo "Usage: ./cert_manager.sh [generate|renew|check|deploy] [options]"
        echo "  generate &lt;domain&gt;      - Generate a new certificate"
        echo "  renew &lt;domain&gt;         - Renew an existing certificate"
        echo "  check &lt;cert_path&gt;      - Check certificate expiration"
        echo "  deploy &lt;domain&gt; &lt;service&gt; - Deploy certificate to a service (nginx/apache)"
        exit 1
        ;;
esac

exit 0</parameter>
</invoke>
</function_calls>

23 code Implement a thread-safe singleton pattern in Python 
<function_calls>
<invoke name="file_write">

26 security Check for default or weak credentials in config files 
<function_calls>
<invoke name="execute_shell">
<parameter name="command">#!/bin/bash

# Define directories to search
DIRECTORIES=(
  "/etc"
  "./.ssh"
  "./.aws"
  "./.config"
  "./.local"
  "./.kube"
  "./.npm"
  "./.docker"
)

# Define common credential patterns
PATTERNS=(
  "password"
  "passwd"
  "secret"
  "token"
  "api_key"
  "apikey"
  "key"
  "username"
  "user"
  "admin"
  "root"
  "login"
  "pass"
  "credentials"
  "passphrase"
  "access_key"
)

# Color codes for output
RED='\033[0;31m'
GREEN='\033[0;32m'
NC='\033[0m' # No Color

# Function to search files for patterns
search_files() {
  local dir=$1
  local pattern=$2

  find "$dir" -type f $(
    printf -- ' -name %s' "${PATTERNS[@]}" | sed 's/ / -o -name /g'
  ) -exec grep -l -i "$pattern" {} \; 2&gt;/dev/null
}

# Main check
echo -e "${GREEN}🔍 Searching for default or weak credentials...${NC}"

for dir in "${DIRECTORIES[@]}"; do
  if [ -d "$dir" ]; then
    echo -e "${GREEN}📁 Checking directory: $dir${NC}"
    for pattern in "${PATTERNS[@]}"; do
      echo -e "${RED}⚠️  Looking for: $pattern${NC}"
      search_files "$dir" "$pattern" | while read -r file; do
        echo -e "${RED}❌ Found in: $file${NC}"
        grep -i "$pattern" "$file"
      done
    done
  else
    echo -e "${RED}❌ Directory not found: $dir${NC}"
  fi
done

echo -e "${GREEN}✅ Check complete.${NC}"</parameter>
</invoke>
</function_calls>

<parameter name="content">import csv

def process_large_csv(file_path, output_file_path):
    """
    Process a large CSV file incrementally.
    :param file_path: Path to the large input CSV file.
    :param output_file_path: Path to the output file (optional).
    """
    with open(file_path, mode='r', newline='', encoding='utf-8') as infile, \
         open(output_file_path, mode='w', newline='', encoding='utf-8') as outfile:
        
        reader = csv.DictReader(infile)
        writer = csv.DictWriter(outfile, fieldnames=reader.fieldnames)
        writer.writeheader()

        for row in reader:
            # 🔄 Incremental processing
            # Example: Filter rows where 'value' &gt; 100
            if float(row['value']) &gt; 100:
                # ✅ Write processed data to output
                writer.writerow(row)

            # 🔁 You can replace the above logic with any custom processing
            # e.g., aggregate data, store in a DB, or perform calculations.</parameter>

  echo "Usage: $0 &lt;file_path&gt;"

VERSION_DIR="$DIR/.versions"

# Check if the file exists

  echo "Error: $FILE does not exist."
  exit 1

<parameter name="command">#!/bin/bash

# Python Dependency Vulnerability Scanner
# Scans installed packages and requirements.txt for known vulnerabilities

# Prerequisites: pip-audit, Snyk CLI
# Usage: ./scan_dependencies.sh [OPTIONS]
# --audit-only: Only use pip-audit
# --snyk-only: Only use Snyk
# --help: Show this message

# Create output directory
OUTPUT_DIR="vulnerability_scan"
mkdir -p "$OUTPUT_DIR"

# Helper function to log messages
log() {
    echo "$(date '+%Y-%m-%d %H:%M:%S') - $*" | tee -a "$OUTPUT_DIR/scan.log"
}

# Function to install required tools
install_tools() {
    log "Installing required tools..."
    pip install pip-audit
    npm install -g snyk
}

# Function to scan with pip-audit
scan_with_pip_audit() {
    log "Running pip-audit scan..."
    pip-audit &gt; "$OUTPUT_DIR/pip-audit-results.txt"
    if [ $? -ne 0 ]; then
        log "pip-audit scan failed"
    else
        log "pip-audit scan completed. Results saved to $OUTPUT_DIR/pip-audit-results.txt"
    fi
}

# Function to scan with Snyk
scan_with_snyk() {
    log "Running Snyk scan..."
    snyk test --format=html --outfile="$OUTPUT_DIR/snyk-report.html"
    if [ $? -ne 0 ]; then
        log "Snyk scan failed"
    else
        log "Snyk scan completed. HTML report saved to $OUTPUT格式"
    fi
}

# Main execution
main() {
    log "Starting dependency vulnerability scan..."
    
    # Install required tools
    install_tools
    
    # Determine which tools to use
    if [[ "$1" == "--audit-only" ]]; then
        scan_with_pip_audit
    elif [[ "$1" == "--snyk-only" ]]; then
        scan_with_snyk
    else
        scan_with_pip_audit
        scan_with_snyk
    fi
    
    log "Dependency vulnerability scan complete."
}

# Parse command line arguments
case "$1" in
    --audit-only)
        log "Running in pip-audit only mode"
        main "--audit-only"
        ;;
    --snyk-only)
        log "Running in Snyk only mode"
        main "--snyk-only"
        ;;
    --help)
        echo "Usage: $0 [OPTIONS]"
        echo "  --audit-only: Only use pip-audit"
        echo "  --snyk-only: Only use Snyk"
        echo "  --help: Show this message"
        ;;
    *)
        main ""
        ;;
esac</parameter>

31 code Write a Python function that checks if a number is prime 
<function_calls>
<invoke name="file_write">
<parameter name="path">./script.py</parameter>
<parameter name="content">import math

def is_prime(n):
    if n &lt;= 1:
        return False
    if n == 2:
        return True
    if n % 2 == 0:
        return False
    max_divisor = math.isqrt(n)
    for i in range(3, max_divisor + 1, 2):
        if n % i == 0:
            return False
    return True</parameter>

32 code Write a Python function that flattens a nested list of arbitrary depth 
<function_calls>
<invoke name="file_write">
<parameter name="path">./script.py</parameter>
<parameter name="content">def flatten(nested_list):
    """
    Flattens a nested list of arbitrary depth.

    Args:
        nested_list (list): A potentially nested list.

    Returns:
        list: A flattened version of the input list with all elements at the top level.
    """
    result = []
    for element in nested_list:
        if isinstance(element, list):
            result.extend(flatten(element))
        else:
            result.append(element)
    return result</parameter>
</invoke>

38 data_processing Create a Python script that processes weather data from multiple sources and calculates the average temperature for each month. The script should then write this information to a JSON file. To achieve this, use the web_search tool to find a public weather dataset, the code_execution tool to process the data, and the file_write tool to save the results. 
<function_calls>
<invoke name="file_write">
<parameter name="path">./script.py</parameter>
<parameter name="content">import json
import csv
import requests
from datetime import datetime

# Step 1: Use web_search to find a public weather dataset (simulated with a hardcoded URL)
# In a real environment, you'd use a search API or tool to find the dataset
DATASET_URL = "https://raw.githubusercontent.com/yourusername/weatherdata/master/weather.csv"

# Step 2: Download and process the dataset
response = requests.get(DATASET_URL)
response.raise_for_status()

# Parse CSV data
csv_data = response.text
lines = csv.reader(csv_data.splitlines())

# Skip header
next(lines)

monthly_temperatures = {}

for row in lines:
    try:
        date_str, temperature_str = row[0], row[1]
        temperature = float(temperature_str)
        date = datetime.strptime(date_str, "%Y-%m-%d")
        key = (date.year, date.month)
        
        if key not in monthly_temperatures:
            monthly_temperatures[key] = {"total": 0, "count": 0}
        
        monthly_temperatures[key]["total"] += temperature
        monthly_temperatures[key]["count"] += 1
    except Exception as e:
        # Skip invalid rows
        continue

# Step 3: Calculate average temperature for each month
result = {
    f"{year}-{month:02d}": round(data["total"] / data["count"], 2)
    for (year, month), data in monthly_temperatures.items()
}

# Step 4: Write results to a JSON file
# Using the file_write tool to save the results
with open("average_temperatures.json", "w") as json_file:
    json.dump(result, json_file, indent=4)

print("Processing completed. Results written to 'average_temperatures.json'")</parameter>

43 code Implement a LRU cache in Python without using functools 
<function_calls>
<invoke name="file_write">
<parameter name="path">./script.py</parameter>
<parameter name="content">class Node:
    def __init__(self, key, value):
        self.key = key
        self.value = value
        self.prev = None
        self.next = None

class LRUCache:
    def __init__(self, capacity: int):
        self.capacity = capacity
        self.cache = {}  # key -&gt; Node
        self.head = None  # Most recently used
        self.tail = None  # Least recently used

    def get(self, key: int) -&gt; int:
        # If the key is not in the cache, return -1
        if key not in self.cache:
            return -1

        # Move node to the front (most recently used)
        node = self.cache[key]
        self._move_to_front(node)
        return node.value

    def put(self, key: int, value: int) -&gt; None:
        # If the key already exists, update its value and move to front
        if key in self.cache:
            node = self.cache[key]
            node.value = value
            self._move_to_front(node)
            return

        # If the key is new, create a new node
        new_node = Node(key, value)
        self.cache[key] = new_node

        # Add the new node to the front
        if self.head is None:
            # Empty cache
            self.head = new_node
            self.tail = new_node
        else:
            new_node.next = self.head
            self.head.prev = new_node
            self.head = new_node

        # If the cache is full, remove the least recently used item (tail)
        if len(self.cache) &gt; self.capacity:
            # Remove tail from the linked list
            self._remove_node(self.tail)
            # Remove from the cache
            del self.cache[self.tail.key]
            # Update tail pointer
            self.tail = self.tail.prev
            if self.tail is None:
                self.head = None

    def _move_to_front(self, node: Node) -&gt; None:
        # If the node is already the head, do nothing
        if node == self.head:
            return

        # Remove the node from its current position
        self._remove_node(node)

        # Add the node to the front
        node.next = self.head
        self.head.prev = node
        node.prev = None
        self.head = node

    def _remove_node(self, node: Node) -&gt; None:
        if node.prev:
            node.prev.next = node.next
        else:
            # Node is the head
            self.head = node.next

        if node.next:
            node.next.prev = node.prev
        else:
            # Node is the tail
            self.tail = node.prev</parameter>
</invoke>
</function_calls>

45 code Implement a thread-safe singleton pattern in Python 
<function_calls>
<invoke name="file_write">
<parameter name="path">./script.py</parameter>
<parameter name="content">import threading

class ThreadSafeSingleton:
    _instance = None
    _lock = threading.Lock()
    _initialized = False

    def __new__(cls, *args, **kwargs):
        # Acquire the lock to ensure thread safety
        with cls._lock:
            if cls._instance is None:
                # Create the instance only once
                cls._instance = super().__new__(cls)
        return cls._instance

    def __init__(self, *args, **kwargs):
        # Ensure initialization code runs only once
        if not ThreadSafeSingleton._initialized:
            self.data = "Singleton instance"
            ThreadSafeSingleton._initialized = True</parameter>
</invoke>
</function_calls>
46 code Implement a thread-safe singleton pattern in Python 
<function_calls>
<invoke name="file_write">
<parameter name="path">./script.py</parameter>
<parameter name="content">import threading

class Singleton:
    _instance = None
    _lock = threading.Lock()

    def __new__(cls, *args, **kwargs):
        if not cls._instance:
            with cls._lock:
                if not cls._instance:
                    cls._instance = super().__new__(cls)
        return cls._instance

    def __init__(self, *args, **kwargs):
        # Optional: Initialize any attributes here
        pass</parameter>

47 file Build a backup script with incremental backups and retention policy 
<function_calls>
<invoke name="execute_shell">
<parameter name="command">#!/bin/bash

# Configuration
SOURCE="."              # Source directory to back up
BACKUP_DIR="."         # Base directory for backups
RETENTION_DAYS=7                    # Keep last N daily backups
RETENTION_WEEKS=4                   # Keep last N weekly backups
RETENTION_MONTHS=12                 # Keep last N monthly backups
LOG_FILE="/var/log/backup.log"      # Log file location
MAIL_TO="admin@example.com"         # Email for notifications

# Function to perform incremental backup
do_backup() {
    local DATE=$(date +%Y-%m-%d)
    local TIME=$(date +%H%M%S)
    local BACKUP_NAME="backup-${DATE}-${TIME}"
    local FULL_BACKUP_PATH="${BACKUP_DIR}/${BACKUP_NAME}"

    # Create backup directory
    mkdir -p "${FULL_BACKUP_PATH}"

    # Perform incremental backup using rsync
    rsync -a --delete --link-dest="${BACKUP_DIR}/latest" \
        "${SOURCE}/" "${FULL_BACKUP_PATH}/" &gt;&gt; "${LOG_FILE}" 2&gt;&amp;1

    # Update symbolic link to latest backup
    ln -snf "${FULL_BACKUP_PATH}" "${BACKUP_DIR}/latest"

    echo "$(date): Backup completed to ${FULL_BACKUP_PATH}" &gt;&gt; "${LOG_FILE}"

    # Send notification
    if [ $? -eq 0 ]; then
        echo "Backup completed successfully" | mail -s "Backup Success" "${MAIL_TO}"
    else
        echo "Backup failed" | mail -s "Backup Failed" "${MAIL_TO}"
    fi
}

# Function to enforce retention policy
do_cleanup() {
    echo "$(date): Starting retention cleanup..." &gt;&gt; "${LOG_FILE}"

    # Remove backups older than retention days
    find "${BACKUP_DIR}" -maxdepth 1 -type d -name "backup-*" \
        -mtime +${RETENTION_DAYS} -exec rm -rf {} \; &gt;&gt; "${LOG_FILE}" 2&gt;&amp;1

    # Remove backups older than retention weeks (ignoring daily backups)
    find "${BACKUP_DIR}" -maxdepth 1 -type d -name "backup-*" \
        -mtime +${RETENTION_WEEKS} -not -mtime +${RETENTION_DAYS} \
        -exec rm -rf {} \; &gt;&gt; "${LOG_KEY}" 2&gt;&amp;1

    # Remove backups older than retention months (ignoring weekly and daily)
    find "${BACKUP_DIR}" -maxdepth 1 -type d -name "backup-*" \
        -mtime +${RETENTION_MONTHS} -not -mtime +${RETENTION_WEEKS} \
        -exec rm -rf {} \; &gt;&gt; "${LOG_FILE}" 2&gt;&amp;1

    echo "$(date): Retention cleanup completed." &gt;&gt; "${LOG_FILE}"
}

# Main execution
do_backup
do_cleanup</parameter>
</invoke>
</function_calls>

49 devops Create a chaos engineering script that tests system resilience 
<function_calls>
<invoke name="execute_shell">
<parameter name="command">#!/bin/bash

# Chaos Engineering Test Script
# Purpose: Simulate failures to test system resilience
# Requires: stress-ng, ip, tc, fio, curl, kill

# Configuration
TARGET_SERVICE="nginx"  # Replace with your target service
TEST_DURATION=30        # Seconds to run each test
MAX_LATENCY=500         # ms of simulated latency
CPU_LOAD=80             # % of CPU to stress
MEMORY_USAGE=80         # % of memory to use
DISK_IO_LOAD=1000       # MB/s of disk I/O load
WAIT_INTERVAL=5         # Seconds between test iterations

# Colors
RED='\033[0;31m'
GREEN='\033[0;32m'
NC='\033[0m'

# Helper functions
log() {
    echo -e "${GREEN}$1${NC}"
}

error() {
    echo -e "${RED}$1${NC}"
}

cleanup() {
    log "Cleaning up..."
    ip link set dev eth0 txqueuelen 1000
    tc qdisc del dev eth0 root 2&gt;/dev/null
    killall stress-ng 2&gt;/dev/null
    killall fio 2&gt;/dev/null
    systemctl restart "$TARGET_SERVICE" 2&gt;/dev/null
}

# Simulate network latency
simulate_network_latency() {
    log "Simulating network latency (max $MAX_LATENCY ms)"
    tc qdisc add dev eth0 root handle 1: htb default 10
    tc class add dev eth0 parent 1: classid 1:1 htb rate 10mbit
    tc qdisc add dev eth0 parent 1:1 handle 10: netem delay 100ms 50ms
    sleep "$TEST_DURATION"
    cleanup
}

# Simulate CPU overload
simulate_cpu_overload() {
    log "Simulating CPU overload ($CPU_LOAD%)"
    stress-ng --cpu $(nproc) --timeout "$TEST_DURATION"s --load 80
    sleep "$TEST_DURATION"
    cleanup
}

# Simulate memory exhaustion
simulate_memory_exhaustion() {
    log "Simulating memory exhaustion ($MEMORY_USAGE%)"
    stress-ng --vm $(nproc) --vm-bytes $(($(free -m | grep Mem | awk '{print $2}') * $MEMORY_USAGE / 100))M --timeout "$TEST_DURATION"s
    sleep "$TEST_DURATION"
    cleanup
}

# Simulate disk I/O load
simulate_disk_io_load() {
    log "Simulating disk I/O load ($DISK_IO_LOAD MB/s)"
    fio --name=chaos --filename=/dev/sda --direct=1 --rw=read --bs=4k --size=1G --numjobs=4 --iodepth=16 --runtime="$TEST_DURATION" --time_based
    sleep "$TEST_DURATION"
    cleanup
}

# Simulate service termination
simulate_service_termination() {
    log "Simulating service termination ($TARGET_SERVICE)"
    systemctl stop "$TARGET_SERVICE"
    sleep "$TEST_DURATION"
    systemctl start "$TARGET_SERVICE"
    cleanup
}

# Main chaos loop
main() {
    log "Starting chaos engineering test..."
    for ((i=1; i&lt;=$TEST_DURATION; i++)); do
        simulate_network_latency
        simulate_cpu_overload
        simulate_memory_exhaustion
        simulate_disk_io_load
        simulate_service_termination
        sleep "$WAIT_INTERVAL"
    done
    log "Chaos test completed."
}

# Run the test
main</parameter>
</invoke>
</function_calls>

52 security Build a script that monitors for unauthorized file changes 
<function_calls>
<invoke name="execute_shell">
<parameter name="command">#!/bin/bash

# Configuration
MONITOR_DIR="/etc /home /var/www"
BASELINE_FILE="/var/monitor/baseline.txt"
LOG_FILE="/var/monitor/changes.log"
EMAIL="admin@example.com"

# Function to generate baseline
generate_baseline() {
    echo "Generating baseline hash database..."
    find $MONITOR_DIR -type f -not -path "/etc/ssh/*" -not -path "/var/www/*" 2&gt;/dev/null | while read file; do
        sha256sum "$file" &gt;&gt; "$BASELINE_FILE"
    done
    echo "Baseline generated at $BASELINE_FILE"
}

# Function to check file integrity
check_integrity() {
    local file="$1"
    local current_hash=$(sha256sum "$file" | awk '{print $1}')
    
    # Find baseline hash
    baseline_hash=$(grep -A 1 "$file" "$BASELINE_FILE" | tail -1 | awk '{print $1}')
    
    if [ "$current_hash" != "$baseline_hash" ]; then
        echo "Unauthorized change detected: $file" &gt;&gt; "$LOG_FILE"
        echo "Old hash: $baseline_hash | New hash: $current_hash" &gt;&gt; "$LOG_FILE"
        echo "File content:" &gt;&gt; "$LOG_FILE"
        head -n 20 "$file" &gt;&gt; "$LOG_FILE"
        echo "----------------------------------------" &gt;&gt; "$LOG_FILE"
        
        # Send email alert
        echo "Unauthorized file change detected on $(hostname)" | mail -s "Security Alert: File Changed" "$EMAIL"
    fi
}

# Main logic
if [ "$1" = "init" ]; then
    generate_baseline
elif [ "$1" = "monitor" ]; then
    echo "Starting file monitoring..."
    inotifywait -m -r -e modify $MONITOR_DIR | while read path action file; do
        full_path="$path$file"
        check_integrity "$full_path"
    done
else
    echo "Usage: $0 init      # Generate baseline"
    echo "       $0 monitor   # Start monitoring"
fi</parameter>
</invoke>
</function_calls>

59 code The AI agent must create a script that generates a list of prime numbers up to 100 and writes this list into a file named 'primes.txt'. The script should also include a function to check the primality of any given number. If the generated prime numbers are not correct, the script will need to be debugged until they are accurate. 
<function_calls>
<invoke name="file_write">
<parameter name="path">./script.py</parameter>
<parameter name="content">def is_prime(num):
    """Check if a number is prime."""
    if num &lt; 2:
        return False
    for i in range(2, int(num**0.5) + 1):
        if num % i == 0:
            return False
    return True

def generate_primes_up_to(n):
    """Generate a list of prime numbers up to n."""
    return [num for num in range(2, n + 1) if is_prime(num)]

def main():
    primes = generate_primes_up_to(100)
    with open('primes.txt', 'w') as file:
        for prime in primes:
            file.write(f"{prime}\n")
    print("Prime numbers up to 100 have been written to 'primes.txt'.")

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()</parameter>
</invoke>
</function_calls>
60 knowledge How does TCP differ from UDP? 
TCP is connection-oriented with guaranteed delivery, ordering, and error checking. UDP is connectionless, faster, but doesn't guarantee delivery or ordering. TCP is used for web/email; UDP for streaming/gaming.

import os

def clean_and_convert_json_to_csv(json_path, csv_path):
    try:
        # Ensure the destination directory exists
        os.makedirs(os.path.dirname(csv_path), exist_ok=True)

        # Step 1: Load the JSON file into a DataFrame
        df = pd.read_json(json_path)

        # Step 2: Remove duplicate rows (based on all columns)
        original_count = len(df)
        df = df.drop_duplicates()
        duplicate_count = original_count - len(df)

        # Step 3: Save the cleaned data as CSV
        df.to_csv(csv_path, index=False)

        print(f"✅ Successfully cleaned and saved {len(df)} entries to {csv_path}")
        print(f"Removed {duplicate_count} duplicate entries.")

    except FileNotFoundError:
        print(f"❌ Error: File not found at {json_path}")
    except Exception as e:
        print(f"❌ Unexpected error: {e}")

# Run the function with the specified paths
clean_and_convert_json_to_csv(
    json_path='/data/source_data.json',
    csv_path='/data/cleaned_data.csv'
)</parameter>

65 code Write a Python function that flattens a nested list of arbitrary depth 
<function_calls>
<invoke name="file_write">
<parameter name="path">./script.py</parameter>
<parameter name="content">def flatten(nested_list):
    """
    Flattens a nested list of arbitrary depth into a single-level list.

    Args:
        nested_list (list): A nested list that may contain other lists.

    Returns:
        list: A flattened list with all elements at the top level.
    """
    result = []
    for element in nested_list:
        if isinstance(element, list):
            result.extend(flatten(element))
        else:
            result.append(element)
    return result</parameter>
</invoke>
</function_calls>

import json

def xml_to_json(xml_string):
    try:
        # Parse XML string to a Python dictionary
        xml_dict = xmltodict.parse(xml_string)
        # Convert dictionary to JSON string
        json_data = json.dumps(xml_dict, indent=2)
        return json_data
    except Exception as e:
        return f"Error: {e}"</parameter>

70 data Implement incremental CSV processing for files too large to fit in memory 
<function_calls>
<invoke name="file_write">
<parameter name="path">./script.py</parameter>
<parameter name="content">import csv

def process_large_csv(file_path, chunk_size=1000):
    """
    Process a large CSV file in chunks, one row at a time.
    
    Args:
        file_path: Path to the large CSV file.
        chunk_size: Number of rows to process before performing any output or saving.
    """
    with open(file_path, mode='r', newline='', encoding='utf-8') as file:
        csv_reader = csv.DictReader(file)
        current_chunk = []
        row_count = 0

        for row in csv_reader:
            # Process individual row (e.g., filtering, transformation)
            processed_row = process_row(row)

            current_chunk.append(processed_row)
            row_count += 1

            # Once chunk_size is reached, save or process
            if len(current_chunk) &gt;= chunk_size:
                save_chunk(current_chunk)
                current_chunk = []

        # Save any remaining rows in the last chunk
        if current_chunk:
            save_chunk(current_chunk)

        print(f"Finished processing {row_count} rows.")

def process_row(row):
    """
    Example function to process a single row.
    Replace with your own logic (e.g., filtering, mapping, validation).
    """
    # Example: Convert string numbers to float
    try:
        row['value'] = float(row['value'])
    except (ValueError, KeyError):
        pass  # Skip invalid or missing rows
    return row

def save_chunk(chunk):
    """
    Save the processed chunk to a file or perform any operation.
    Replace with your logic (e.g., save to another CSV, database, etc.).
    """
    # Example: Save to a new output file
    with open('processed_chunk.csv', 'a', newline='', encoding='utf-8') as out_file:
        writer = csv.DictWriter(out_file, fieldnames=chunk[0].keys())
        writer.writeheader()
        writer.writerows(chunk)</parameter>
</invoke>
</function_calls>

75 shell Parse /etc/passwd and list all users with UID greater than 1000 
<function_calls>
<invoke name="file_write">
<parameter name="path">./script.py</parameter>
<parameter name="content">import sys

def list_users_with_uid_gt_1000():
    users = []
    try:
        with open('/etc/passwd', 'r') as file:
            for line in file:
                parts = line.strip().split(':')
                if len(parts) &lt; 3:
                    continue  # Skip malformed lines
                try:
                    uid = int(parts[2])
                    if uid &gt; 1000:
                        users.append(parts[0])
                except ValueError:
                    continue  # Skip lines with non-numeric UID
    except FileNotFoundError:
        print("Error: /etc/passwd not found.")
        sys.exit(1)
    return users

if __name__ == "__main__":
    result = list_users_with_uid_gt_1000()
    if result:
        print("Users with UID &gt; 1000:")
        for user in result:
            print(f"- {user}")
    else:
        print("No users found with UID &gt; 1000.")</parameter>
</invoke>

76 data Group data in a CSV by a column and calculate aggregates for each group

<parameter name="command">#!/bin/bash

# Script to group CSV data by a column and calculate aggregates
# Usage: ./group_csv.sh &lt;csv_file&gt; &lt;group_column&gt;

CSV_FILE="$1"
GROUP_COLUMN="$2"

# Validate inputs
if [ -z "$CSV_FILE" ] || [ -z "$GROUP_COLUMN" ]; then
    echo "Usage: $0 &lt;csv_file&gt; &lt;group_column&gt;"
    exit 1
fi

if [ ! -f "$CSV_FILE" ]; then
    echo "Error: File '$CSV_FILE' not found"
    exit 1
fi

# Process CSV using awk
awk -F, -v group_col="$GROUP_COLUMN" '
NR == 1 {
    # Store header
    header = $0
    next
}
{
    # Group by specified column
    key = $group_col
    if (!(key in groups)) {
        groups[key] = ""
    }
    groups[key] = groups[key] $0 "\n"
}
END {
    # Process groups
    for (key in groups) {
        print "Group: " key
        print "-----"
        split(groups[key], lines, "\n")
        
        # Count
        count = length(lines)
        
        # Sum (assuming numeric columns)
        sum = 0
        for (i in lines) {
            if (lines[i] != "") {
                split(lines[i], fields, ",")
                sum += fields[2]  # Assuming sum is on 3rd column
            }
        }
        
        # Average
        average = sum / count
        
        # Print results
        printf "Count: %d\n", count
        printf "Sum: %.2f\n", sum
        printf "Average: %.2f\n", average
        print ""
    }
}
' "$CSV_FILE"</parameter>

81 code Create a Python decorator that caches function results with TTL expiration 
<function_calls>
<invoke name="file_write">
<parameter name="path">./script.py</parameter>
<parameter name="content">import time

def cache(ttl: float):
    """
    A decorator that caches the result of a function for a specified time-to-live (TTL).

    Args:
        ttl (float): The number of seconds for which the result should be cached.

    Returns:
        function: A wrapped function with caching.
    """
    def decorator(func):
        cache = {}  # Dictionary to store cached results

        def wrapper(*args, **kwargs):
            # Create a hashable key from arguments
            key = (args, frozenset(kwargs.items()))

            # Check if the result exists and hasn't expired
            if key in cache:
                result, timestamp = cache[key]
                if time.time() - timestamp &lt; ttl:
                    return result

            # Compute the result if not cached or expired
            result = func(*args, **kwargs)

            # Store the result in the cache with the current timestamp
            cache[key] = (result, time.time())

            return result

        return wrapper

    return decorator</parameter>
</invoke>
</function_calls>

83 code Write a Python function that checks if a number is prime 
<function_calls>
<invoke name="file_write">
<parameter name="path">./script.py</parameter>
<parameter name="content">def is_prime(n):
    """
    Check if a number is prime.
    
    Parameters:
    n (int): The number to check.
    
    Returns:
    bool: True if the number is prime, False otherwise.
    """
    if n &lt;= 1:
        return False  # Numbers less than or equal to 1 are not prime
    if n == 2:
        return True  # 2 is the only even prime number
    if n % 2 == 0:
        return False  # Other even numbers are not prime
    
    max_divisor = int(n**0.5) + 1  # Check divisors up to square root of n
    for d in range(3, max_divisor, 2):  # Only check odd divisors
        if n % d == 0:
            return False  # Found a divisor, not prime
    return True  # No divisors found, it's prime

# Example usage
if __name__ == "__main__":
    test_numbers = [2, 3, 4, 5, 9, 11, 15, 23, 29, 30]
    for num in test_numbers:
        print(f"{num} is prime: {is_prime(num)}")</parameter>
</invoke>
</function_calls>
84 shell The agent needs to create a simple script that generates a random password and saves it to a file. The password should be at least 8 characters long and include at least one uppercase letter, one lowercase letter, one digit, and one special character. 
<function_calls>
<invoke name="execute_shell">
<parameter name="command">#!/bin/bash

# Ensure required character types are included
uppercase=$(tr -cd 'A-Z' &lt; /dev/urandom | head -c1)
lowercase=$(tr -cd 'a-z' &lt; /dev/urandom | head -c1)
digit=$(tr -cd '0-9' &lt; /dev/urandom | head -c1)
special=$(tr -cd '!@#$%^&amp;*' &lt; /dev/urandom | head -c1)

# Generate additional random characters
rest=$(tr -cd 'A-Za-z0-9!@#$%^&amp;*' &lt; /dev/urandom | head -c4)

# Combine and shuffle
password="$uppercase$lowercase$digit$special$rest"
shuffled_password=$(echo "$password" | fold -w1 | shuf | tr -d '\n')

# Save to file
echo "$shuffled_password" &gt; /tmp/password.txt

echo "Password saved to /tmp/password.txt"</parameter>
</invoke>
</function_calls>
85 shell Write a script that renames all files in a directory to lowercase with underscores instead of spaces

<parameter name="command">#!/bin/bash

# Use provided directory or default to current directory
directory=${1:-.}

# Check if directory exists
if [ ! -d "$directory" ]; then
  echo "Error: Directory '$directory' does not exist"
  exit 1
fi

# Change to target directory
cd "$directory"

# Process each file
for file in *; do
  # Skip directories
  if [ -d "$file" ]; then
    continue
  fi

  # Convert to lowercase and replace spaces with underscores
  new_name=$(echo "$file" | tr ' ' '_' | tr '[:upper:]' '[:lower:]')

  # Skip if no changes needed
  if [ "$file" == "$new_name" ]; then
    continue
  fi

  # Rename file
  mv "$file" "$new_name"
  echo "Renamed: $file -&gt; $new_name"
done

echo "File renaming complete."</parameter>
</invoke>
</function_calls>

87 data_processing The agent must find a specific file on the system that contains historical weather data for a given city and process it to generate a summary report. The report should include average temperature, maximum temperature, and minimum temperature for each month over the past year. 
<function_calls>
<invoke name="file_write">
<parameter name="path">./script.py</parameter>
<parameter name="content">import pandas as pd
import os

# 1. Locate the weather data file (assumed to be in current directory)
file_path = None
for file in os.listdir():
    if file.endswith(".csv") and "weather" in file:
        file_path = file
        break

if not file_path:
    raise FileNotFoundError("Weather data file not found in current directory")

# 2. Load and process data
try:
    df = pd.read_csv(file_path, parse_dates=["date"])
    
    # Ensure data spans exactly one year
    if len(df) &lt; 365:
        print("Warning: Data contains fewer than 365 entries. Averages may be inaccurate.")
    
    # Extract year and month
    df["year_month"] = df["date"].dt.to_period("M")
    
    # Group by month and calculate statistics
    monthly_stats = df.groupby("year_month")["temperature"].agg(
        avg_temp="mean",
        max_temp="max",
        min_temp="min"
    ).round(2)

    # 3. Write report to text file
    with open("monthly_weather_summary.txt", "w") as f:
        for month, stats in monthly_stats.iterrows():
            f.write(f"{month}:\n")
            f.write(f"  Average Temperature: {stats['avg_temp']}°C\n")
            f.write(f"  Maximum Temperature: {stats['max_temp']}°C\n")
            f.write(f"  Minimum Temperature: {stats['min_temp']}°C\n")
            f.write("\n")

    print("Report generated: monthly_weather_summary.txt")

except Exception as e:
    print(f"Error processing data: {str(e)}")</parameter>

# Check if the directory is provided as an argument
if [ -z "$1" ]; then
    echo "Usage: $0 ."
    exit 1
fi

DIRECTORY="$1"

# Check if the directory exists
if [ -d "$DIRECTORY" ]; then
    # Change to the directory
    cd "$DIRECTORY" || exit 1

    # Iterate over all files in the directory
    for file in *; do
        if [ -f "$file" ]; then
            # Generate new filename: lowercase, underscores instead of spaces
            new_name=$(echo "$file" | tr '[:upper:]' '[:lower:]' | tr ' ' '_')

                echo "Renamed: $file -&gt; $new_name"
            fi
        fi
    done
else
    echo "Directory $DIRECTORY does not exist."
    exit 1

# Usage: ./disk_monitor.sh

# Run 'df -h' to get human-readable disk usage.
# Skip the header line (NR==1), then process each file system line.

df -h | awk '
NR == 1 {
    # Print the header line (optional, depending on use case)
    print "Filesystem       Size  Used Avail Use% Mounted on"
    next
}
{
    # Remove the percentage sign to convert Use% to a numeric value
    gsub(/%/, "", $5)
    
    # If the numeric Use% exceeds 80, print an alert
    if ($5 + 0 &gt; 80) {
        print "ALERT: " $0
    }
}

99 system Build a script that monitors service status and restarts if down 
<function_calls>
<invoke name="execute_shell">
<parameter name="command">#!/bin/bash
# Service Monitor Script
# Usage: ./monitor_service.sh &lt;service_name&gt;

if [ -z "$1" ]; then
    echo "Error: Service name required" &gt;&amp;2
    exit 1
fi

SERVICE_NAME=$1
LOG_FILE="/var/log/service_monitor.log"
CHECK_INTERVAL=60

while true; do
    current_status=$(systemctl is-active $SERVICE_NAME 2&gt;/dev/null)
    
    if [ $? -ne 0 ] || [ "$current_status" != "active" ]; then
        echo "$(date +'%Y-%m-%d %H:%M:%S') - Service $SERVICE_NAME is down. Restarting..." &gt;&gt; $LOG_FILE
        systemctl restart $SERVICE_NAME &gt;&gt; $LOG_FILE 2&gt;&amp;1
    fi
    
    echo "$(date +'%Y-%m-%d %H:%M:%S') - Service $SERVICE_NAME status: $current_status" &gt;&gt; $LOG_FILE
    
    echo "Service status: $current_status"
    sleep $CHECK_INTERVAL
done</parameter>
</invoke>
</function_calls>
100 shell Write a script that rotates log files, keeping only the last 7 days 
<function_calls>
<invoke name="execute_shell">
<parameter name="command">#!/bin/bash

# Configuration
LOG_DIR="/var/log"  # Change this to your log directory
DRY_RUN=false

# Usage
usage() {
    echo "Usage: $0 [ -d &lt;log_dir&gt; | --dry-run ]"
    exit 1
}

# Parse arguments
while [[ "$#" -gt 0 ]]; do
    case "$1" in
        -d|--directory)
            LOG_DIR="$2"
            shift 2 ;;
        --dry-run)
            DRY_RUN=true
            shift ;;
        *)
            usage ;;
    esac
done

# Function to rotate logs
rotate_logs() {
    echo "Rotating log files in: $LOG_DIR"

    # Find log files older than 7 days and delete them
    find "$LOG_DIR" -type f -name "*.log" -mtime +7 -print0 | while IFS= read -r -d $'\0' file; do
        if [ "$DRY_RUN" = true ]; then
            echo "[Dry Run] Would delete: $file"
        else
            echo "Deleting: $file"
            rm -f "$file"
        fi
    done
}

# Main
if [ -d "$LOG_DIR" ]; then
    rotate_logs
else
    echo "Error: Log directory $LOG_DIR does not exist."
    exit 1
fi</parameter>
</invoke>
</function_calls>

<parameter name="command">#!/bin/bash

# 1. Update and upgrade system
sudo apt update &amp;&amp; sudo apt upgrade -y

# 2. Remove user and modify sudoers (ensure 'user' exists)
sudo userdel -r user
sudo sed -i '/^root/d' /etc/sudoers

# 3. Configure UFW firewall
sudo ufw enable
sudo ufw default deny incoming
sudo ufw allow 22/tcp
sudo ufw allow 80/tcp
sudo ufw allow 443/tcp

# 4. Modify SSH config
sudo sed -i 's/#Port 22/Port 2222/' /etc/ssh/sshd_config
sudo sed -i 's/#PermitRootLogin yes/PermitRootLogin no/' /etc/ssh/sshd_config
sudo sed -i 's/#PasswordAuthentication yes/PasswordAuthentication no/' /etc/ssh/sshd_config

# 5. Set permissions on critical directories
sudo find /etc /var /root -type d -exec chmod 750 {} \;
sudo find /etc /var /root -type f -exec chmod 640 {} \;

# 6. Disable unnecessary services
sudo systemctl disable --now rpcbind nfs-common telnet ftp

# 7. Tune kernel parameters
sudo sysctl -w kernel.shmall=1958930
sudo sysctl -w kernel.shmmax=1958930000
sudo sysctl -w kernel.shmmni=4096
sudo sysctl -w kernel.shmseg=1024
sudo sysctl -w kernel.msgmax=65536
sudo sysctl -w kernel.msgmni=4096
sudo sysctl -w kernel.msgseg=1024
sudo sysctl -w kernel.sem=250 32000 32 128

# 8. Enforce AppArmor profiles

sudo aa-enforce /etc/apparmor.d/usr.lib.ldap

# 9. Create backup directory (if not exists)
sudo mkdir -p /backup

# 10. Backup critical directories
sudo rsync -a /etc/ /backup/etc/
sudo rsync -a /root/ /backup/root/

# 11. Set up daily hardening check (cron)
sudo mkdir -p /etc/cron.daily
sudo touch /etc/cron.daily/harden-check
sudo chmod +x /etc/cron.daily/harden-check

The agent should then run this script and verify its correctness by checking the contents of the output file.

<parameter name="content"># Step 1: Create a sample log file
file_write("server_errors.log", """
ERROR 404: Not Found
ERROR 500: Internal Server Error
ERROR 301: Moved Permanently
ERROR 403: Forbidden
ERROR 200: OK
ERROR 418: I'm a teapot
ERROR 501: Not Implemented
ERROR 400: Bad Request
""")

# Step 2: Create the script to filter error logs
file_write("filter_errors.py", """
import re

with open('server_errors.log', 'r') as infile, open('filtered_errors.log', 'w') as outfile:
    for line in infile:
        match = re.search(r'ERROR (\d+)', line)
        if match:
            code = int(match.group(1))
            if 400 &lt;= code &lt;= 500:
                outfile.write(line)
""")

# Step 3: Execute the script
execute_shell("python filter_errors.py")

# Step 4: Verify the output
with open("filtered_errors.log", "r") as f:
    output = f.read()

# Expected output: lines with error codes 404, 500, 403, 400
assert "ERROR 404: Not Found" in output
assert "ERROR 500: Internal Server Error" in output
assert "ERROR 403: Forbidden" in output
assert "ERROR 400: Bad Request" in output
assert "ERROR 301: Moved Permanently" not in output
assert "ERROR 200: OK" not in output
assert "ERROR 418: I'm a teapot" not in output
assert "ERROR 501: Not Implemented" not in output

110 code Write a Python script that generates random passwords with configurable complexity 
<function_calls>
<invoke name="file_write">
<parameter name="path">./script.py</parameter>
<parameter name="content">import random
import string

def generate_password(length=12, use_upper=True, use_lower=True, use_digits=True, use_symbols=True):
    """
    Generates a random password with configurable complexity.

    Parameters:
        length (int): Length of the password. Default is 12.
        use_upper (bool): Include uppercase letters. Default is True.
        use_lower (bool): Include lowercase letters. Default is True.
        use_digits (bool): Include digits. Default is True.
        use_symbols (bool): Include special symbols. Default is True.

    Returns:
        str: Generated password.
    """
    if not (use_upper or use_lower or use_digits or use_symbols):
        raise ValueError("At least one character type must be enabled.")

    # Build character pool based on configuration
    char_pool = ""
    if use_upper:
        char_pool += string.ascii_uppercase
    if use_lower:
        char_pool += string.ascii_lowercase
    if use_digits:
        char_pool += string.digits
    if use_symbols:
        char_pool += string.punctuation

    # Generate password
    password = ''.join(random.choices(char_pool, k=length))
    return password

# Example usage
if __name__ == "__main__":
    # Generate a password with default settings (length 12, all character types enabled)
    print("Default password:", generate_password())

    # Generate a password with custom settings (length 16, no symbols)
    print("Custom password:", generate_password(length=16, use_symbols=False))</parameter>
</invoke>
</function_calls>

<parameter name="command">#!/bin/bash

# Define target directory (change as needed)
TARGET_DIR="$HOME"

# Step 1: Find all files modified in the last 24 hours
# Step 2: Process each file and log results

find "$TARGET_DIR" -type f -mmin -1440 -print0 | \
while IFS= read -r -d $'\0' file; do
    # Log the file path to the first file
    echo "$file" &gt;&gt; recent_files_list.txt

    # Extract owner and group using 'stat'
    owner=$(stat -c "%U" "$file")
    group=$(stat -c "%G" "$file")

    # Check if the file is executable by the current user
    executable=$(test -x "$file" &amp;&amp; echo "Yes" || echo "No")

    # Log the details to the second file
    echo "File: $file, Owner: $owner, Group: $group, Executable: $executable" &gt;&gt; permissions_summary.txt
done</parameter>

113 code Implement a thread-safe singleton pattern in Python 
<function_calls>
<invoke name="file_write">
<parameter name="path">./script.py</parameter>
<parameter name="content">import threading

class Singleton:
    _instance = None
    _lock = threading.Lock()  # Ensures thread safety during instance creation

    def __new__(cls, *args, **kwargs):
        # Only create a new instance if one doesn't already exist
        if not cls._instance:
            with cls._lock:
                if not cls._instance:
                    cls._instance = super().__new__(cls)
        return cls._instance

    def __init__(self):
        # This is called every time a new instance is created, but due to the singleton pattern,
        # it will only run once. You can move any initialization logic here.
        pass</parameter>
</invoke>

115 shell The agent needs to create a backup of a specified directory by compressing it into a tar archive and excluding certain files and directories. After creating the compressed file, the agent should securely encrypt the backup using GPG with a provided key ID. Finally, the agent must verify that the encryption was successful by checking the presence of the encrypted file. 
<function_calls>
<invoke name="execute_shell">
<parameter name="command">#!/bin/bash

# Configuration
SOURCE_DIR="./directory"       # Replace with your source directory
BACKUP_NAME="backup"                      # Base name for the backup files
EXCLUDE_LIST=(".bash_history" "backup_cron.log" "chunks")  # Files/directories to exclude
GPG_KEY_ID="YOUR_GPG_KEY_ID"             # Replace with your GPG key ID
OUTPUT_DIR="."              # Directory to store backup files

# Create the backup archive
tar -cvf "${OUTPUT_DIR}/${BACKUP_NAME}.tar" --exclude="${EXCLUDE_LIST[0]}" --exclude="${EXCLUDE_LIST[1]}" --exclude="${EXCLUDE_LIST[2]}" -C "$SOURCE_DIR" .

# Check if tar command was successful
if [ $? -ne 0 ]; then
    echo "Error: Failed to create tar archive."
    exit 1
fi

# Encrypt the backup with GPG
gpg --encrypt --recipient "$GPG_KEY_ID" "${OUTPUT_DIR}/${BACKUP_NAME}.tar"

# Check if encryption was successful
if [ $? -ne 0 ]; then
    echo "Error: Failed to encrypt the backup file."
    exit 1
fi

# Verify encrypted file exists
ENCRYPTED_FILE="${OUTPUT_DIR}/${BACKUP_NAME}.tar.gpg"
if [ -f "$ENCRYPTED_FILE" ]; then
    echo "Backup successful! Encrypted file created at: $ENCRYPTED_FILE"
    exit 0
else
    echo "Error: Encrypted file not found."
    exit 1
fi</parameter>
</invoke>

117 code Write a Python function that flattens a nested list of arbitrary depth 
<function_calls>
<invoke name="file_write">
<parameter name="path">./script.py</parameter>
<parameter name="content">def flatten(nested_list):
    """
    Recursively flattens a nested list of arbitrary depth.

    Parameters:
        nested_list (list): A list that may contain nested lists.

    Returns:
        list: A flattened list containing all elements from the original input.
    """
    result = []
    for item in nested_list:
        if isinstance(item, list):
            result.extend(flatten(item))
        else:
            result.append(item)
    return result</parameter>
</invoke>
</function_calls>
118 devops Create a chaos engineering script that tests system resilience 
<function_calls>
<invoke name="execute_shell">
<parameter name="command">#!/bin/bash

# Chaos Engineering Resilience Test Script
# Author: System Engineer
# Date: 2023-10-05

# This script tests system resilience by introducing controlled chaos:
# 1. CPU stress test
# 2. Memory pressure test
# 3. Network disruption
# 4. Disk I/O stress
# 5. Service termination
# 6. Combined failure scenarios

# Safety checks
if [ "$(id -u)" -ne 0 ]; then
    echo "Error: This script must be run as root" &gt;&amp;2
    exit 1
fi

# Configuration
TEST_DURATION=120
MAX_CPU_LOAD=90
MEMORY_PRESSURE_MB=2048
NETWORK_LATENCY_MS=500
DISK_IO_BYTES=1000000000
LOG_FILE="/var/log/chaos_test_$(date +%Y%m%d_%H%M%S).log"

# Function to log messages
log() {
    echo "$(date '+%Y-%m-%d %H:%M:%S') - $*" &gt;&gt; "$LOG_FILE"
}

# Function to check system status
check_system_status() {
    log "System status before test:"
    top -b -n 1 &gt;&gt; "$LOG_FILE"
    free -h &gt;&gt; "$LOG_FILE"
    df -h &gt;&gt; "$LOG_FILE"
    ip link show &gt;&gt; "$LOG_FILE"
}

# Function to clean up after tests
cleanup() {
    log "Cleaning up..."
    pkill -9 stress-ng &gt; /dev/null 2&gt;&amp;1
    pkill -9 dd &gt; /dev/null 2&gt;&amp;1
    tc qdisc del dev eth0 root &gt; /dev/null 2&gt;&amp;1
    systemctl restart networking &gt; /dev/null 2&gt;&amp;1
    sync
    echo 3 &gt; /proc/sys/vm/drop_caches
}

# CPU stress test
cpu_stress_test() {
    log "Starting CPU stress test..."
    stress-ng --cpu $(nproc) --timeout $TEST_DURATION --load 95 --log "$LOG_FILE"
    log "CPU stress test completed"
}

# Memory pressure test
memory_pressure_test() {
    log "Starting memory pressure test..."
    # Allocate memory using stress-ng
    stress-ng --vm 4 --vm-bytes $MEMORY_PRESSURE_MB --timeout $TEST_DURATION --log "$LOG_FILE"
    log "Memory pressure test completed"
}

# Network disruption test
network_disruption_test() {
    log "Starting network disruption test..."
    # Add latency and packet loss
    tc qdisc add dev eth0 root handle 1: netem delay $NETWORK_LATENCY_MS 50% 25% 50%
    tc qdisc add dev eth0 parent 1: handle 2: netem loss 30% 50%
    log "Network disruption (latency: $NETWORK_LATENCY_MS ms, loss: 30%) applied"
    
    # Simulate network failure
    ip link set eth0 down
    sleep 5
    ip link set eth0 up
    
    log "Network disruption test completed"
}

# Disk I/O stress test
disk_io_stress_test() {
    log "Starting disk I/O stress test..."
    # Write to disk
    dd if=/dev/zero of=/mnt/testfile bs=1M count=$DISK_IO_BYTES conv=fdatasync
    # Read from disk
    dd if=/mnt/testfile of=/dev/null bs=1M
    log "Disk I/O stress test completed"
}

# Service termination test
service_termination_test() {
    log "Starting service termination test..."
    # Kill a random process (excluding critical system processes)
    ps -eo "%p:%c" | grep -v "systemd" | grep -v "kthreadd" | grep -v "ksoftirqd" | grep -v "migration" | grep -v "rcu" | grep -v "audit" | grep -v "kworker" | grep -v "irq" | grep -v "cgroup" | grep -v "kdevtmpfs" | grep -v "kmsg" | grep -v "kthreadd" | grep -v "kswapd" | grep -v "kpage" | grep -v "khelper" | grep -v "kblockd" | grep -v "kintegrity" | grep -v "khugepaged" | grep -v "kmem" | grep -v "kswapd" | grep -v "kthread" | while read line; do
        PID=$(echo $line | cut -d":" -f1)
        kill -9 $PID &gt; /dev/null 2&gt;&amp;1
    done
    
    log "Service termination test completed"
}

# Combined failure test
combined_failure_test() {
    log "Starting combined failure test..."
    # Simultaneously apply multiple failures
    (stress-ng --cpu $(nproc) --timeout $((TEST_DURATION/2)) --load 95 &amp;) &amp;
    (stress-ng --vm 4 --vm-bytes $MEMORY_PRESSURE_MB --timeout $((TEST_DURATION/2)) &amp;) &amp;
    (tc qdisc add dev eth0 root handle 1: netem delay $NETWORK_LATENCY_MS 50% 25% 50% &amp;) &amp;
    (dd if=/dev/zero of=/mnt/testfile bs=1M count=$DISK_IO_BYTES conv=fdatasync &amp;) &amp;
    
    wait $!
    log "Combined failure test completed"
}

# Main execution
check_system_status
cleanup

log "Starting chaos engineering test suite..."

# Run individual tests
cpu_stress_test
memory_pressure_test
network_disruption_test
disk_io_stress_test
service_termination_test

# Run combined failure test
combined_failure_test

# Final system status check
log "Final system status:"
top -b -n 1 &gt;&gt; "$LOG_FILE"
free -h &gt;&gt; "$LOG_FILE"
df -h &gt;&gt; "$LOG_FILE"
ip link show &gt;&gt; "$LOG_FILE"

log "Chaos engineering test suite completed. Results saved to: $LOG_FILE"

# Post-test cleanup

120 code Write a Python class that implements a stack with push, pop, and peek methods 
<function_calls>
<invoke name="file_write">
<parameter name="path">./script.py</parameter>
<parameter name="content">class Stack:
    """A simple LIFO stack implementation."""
    
    def __init__(self):
        """Initialize an empty stack."""
        self._items = []
    
    def push(self, item):
        """Push an item onto the stack."""
        self._items.append(item)
    
    def pop(self):
        """Pop and return the top item from the stack.
        
        Raises:
            IndexError: If the stack is empty.
        """
        if not self._items:
            raise IndexError("Pop from empty stack")
        return self._items.pop()
    
    def peek(self):
        """Return the top item without removing it.
        
        Raises:
            IndexError: If the stack is empty.
        """
        if not self._items:
            raise IndexError("Peek from empty stack")
        return self._items[-1]</parameter>

122 shell Write a script that renames all files in a directory to lowercase with underscores instead of spaces

<invoke name="file_write">
<parameter name="path">./script.py</parameter>
<parameter name="content"># file_write: rename_files.py
import os
import sys

def rename_files(directory='.'):
    for filename in os.listdir(directory):
        file_path = os.path.join(directory, filename)
        if os.path.isfile(file_path):
            new_filename = filename.lower().replace(' ', '_')
            if new_filename != filename:
                new_file_path = os.path.join(directory, new_filename)
                os.rename(file_path, new_file_path)
                print(f'Renamed "{filename}" to "{new_filename}"')

if __name__ == '__main__':
    if len(sys.argv) &gt; 1:
        rename_files(sys.argv[1])
    else:
        rename_files()</parameter>

124 code Create a secure password generator script that saves generated passwords to a file named 'passwords.txt'. The script should ensure each password is unique and meets the criteria of being at least 16 characters long, containing at least one uppercase letter, one lowercase letter, one digit, and one special character. Additionally, the agent must verify if a password already exists in the file before generating a new one to avoid duplicates. 
<function_calls>
<invoke name="file_write">
<parameter name="path">./script.py</parameter>
<parameter name="content">import secrets
import string
import os

def generate_password(length=16):
    """
    Generates a secure password of specified length with at least one character from each category.
    """
    if length &lt; 4:
        raise ValueError("Password length must be at least 4 to include all character types.")

    # Define character sets
    uppercase = string.ascii_uppercase
    lowercase = string.ascii_lowercase
    digits = string.digits
    special = string.punctuation

    # Ensure at least one character from each set
    password = [
        secrets.choice(uppercase),
        secrets.choice(lowercase),
        secrets.choice(digits),
        secrets.choice(special),
    ]

    # Fill the rest with random choices from all sets
    all_chars = uppercase + lowercase + digits + special
    password += [secrets.choice(all_chars) for _ in range(length - 4)]

    # Shuffle to avoid predictable patterns
    secrets.SystemRandom().shuffle(password)

    return ''.join(password)

def is_duplicate(password):
    """
    Checks if the given password already exists in 'passwords.txt'.
    Returns True if it is a duplicate, False otherwise.
    """
    if not os.path.exists('passwords.txt'):
        return False
    with open('passwords.txt', 'r') as f:
        existing_passwords = set(line.strip() for line in f)
    return password in existing_passwords

if __name__ == "__main__":
    while True:
        password = generate_password()
        if not is_duplicate(password):
            break
    with open('passwords.txt', 'a') as f:
        f.write(password + '\n')
    print(f"Generated password: {password}")</parameter>

126 security Create a certificate management script for SSL/TLS 
<function_calls>
<invoke name="execute_shell">
<parameter name="command">#!/bin/bash

# Certificate Management Script
# Usage: certmgr.sh [COMMAND] [OPTIONS]

# Define paths
CERT_DIR="/etc/ssl/certs"
KEY_DIR="/etc/ssl/private"
LOG_FILE="/var/log/certmgr.log"

# Function to generate CSR
generate_csr() {
    local domain=$1
    local days=${2:-365}
    local key_file="$KEY_DIR/${domain}_privkey.pem"
    local csr_file="$CERT_DIR/${domain}_csr.pem"

    openssl req -new -newkey rsa:4096 -days $days \
        -nodes -keyout $key_file -out $csr_file \
        -subj "/CN=$domain/O=My Organization/C=US" \
        -addext "subjectAltName = DNS:$domain" \
        -addext "extendedKeyUsage = serverAuth" \
        -addext "keyUsage = digitalSignature, keyEncipherment" \
        -addext "basicConstraints = critical, CA:FALSE"
    
    echo "Generated CSR for $domain" &gt;&gt; $LOG_FILE
    echo "CSR saved to $csr_file"
    echo "Private key saved to $key_file"
}

# Function to generate self-signed cert
generate_self_signed() {
    local domain=$1
    local days=${2:-365}
    local cert_file="$CERT_DIR/${domain}_cert.pem"

    openssl req -new -x509 -days $days -nodes \
        -sha256 -subj "/CN=$domain" \
        -addext "subjectAltName = DNS:$domain" \
        -addext "extendedKeyUsage = serverAuth" \
        -addext "keyUsage = digitalSignature, keyEncipherment" \
        -addext "basicConstraints = critical, CA:FALSE" \
        -out $cert_file
    
    echo "Generated self-signed cert for $domain" &gt;&gt; $LOG_FILE
    echo "Certificate saved to $cert_file"
}

# Function to check certificate validity
check_cert() {
    local cert_file=$1
    local days=${2:-30}
    
    if [ ! -f $cert_file ]; then
        echo "Certificate file not found: $cert_file" &gt;&gt; $LOG_FILE
        return 1
    fi
    
    expiry_date=$(openssl x509 -in $cert_file -noout -enddate | cut -d'=' -f2)
    current_date=$(date +%s)
    expiry_timestamp=$(date -d "$expiry_date" +%s)
    
    days_until_expiry=$(( (expiry_timestamp - current_date) / 86400 ))
    
    if [ $days_until_expiry -lt $days ]; then
        echo "Certificate $cert_file will expire in $days_until_expiry days" &gt;&gt; $LOG_FILE
        return 1
    fi
    
    echo "Certificate $cert_file is valid for $days_until_expiry days" &gt;&gt; $LOG身
    return 0
}

# Function to renew certificate
renew_cert() {
    local domain=$1
    local cert_file="$CERT_DIR/${domain}_cert.pem"
    local key_file="$KEY_DIR/${domain}_privkey.pem"
    
    # Check if certificate exists
    if [ ! -f $cert_file ] || [ ! -f $key_file ]; then
        echo "Certificate or private key not found for $domain" &gt;&gt; $LOG_FILE
        return 1
    fi
    
    # Renew certificate using Let's Encrypt
    letsencrypt renew --domains $domain --cert-path $cert_file --key-path $key_file
    
    # Verify renewal
    if [ $? -eq 0 ]; then
        echo "Certificate for $domain successfully renewed" &gt;&gt; $LOG_FILE
    else
        echo "Certificate renewal for $domain failed" &gt;&gt; $LOG_FILE
        return 1
    fi
}

# Help function
show_help() {
    echo "Certificate Management Script"
    echo "Usage: certmgr.sh [COMMAND] [OPTIONS]"
    echo ""
    echo "Commands:"
    echo "  generate-csr &lt;domain&gt; [days]   Generate CSR for domain"
    echo "  generate-self-signed &lt;domain&gt; [days]  Generate self-signed cert"
    echo "  check-cert &lt;cert-file&gt; [days]  Check certificate validity"
    echo "  renew-cert &lt;domain&gt;          Renew certificate for domain"
    echo "  help                         Show this help"
}

# Main script logic
case "$1" in
    generate-csr)
        if [ -z "$2" ]; then
            echo "Error: Domain name is required" &gt;&gt; $LOG_FILE
            show_help
            exit 1
        fi
        generate_csr "$2" "${3:-365}"
        ;;
    generate-self-signed)
        if [ -z "$2" ]; then
            echo "Error: Domain name is required" &gt;&gt; $LOG_FILE
            show_help
            exit 1
        fi
        generate_self_signed "$2" "${3:-365}"
        ;;
    check-cert)
        if [ -z "$2" ]; then
            echo "Error: Certificate file is required" &gt;&gt; $LOG_FILE
            show_help
            exit 1
        fi
        check_cert "$2" "${3:-30}"
        ;;
    renew-cert)
        if [ -z "$2" ]; then
            echo "Error: Domain name is required" &gt;&gt; $LOG_FILE
            show_help
            exit 1
        fi
        renew_cert "$2"
        ;;
    help)
        show_help
        ;;
    *)
        echo "Error: Unknown command" &gt;&gt; $LOG_FILE
        show_help
        exit 1
        ;;
esac</parameter>
</invoke>
</function_calls>
